build.sh:23231:   " **Type fills its bubble (fix #17 T1).** For every display, figure, or label type set inside a bounded container — a tile, card, bubble, chip, tag, badge, stat fill, or price-ladder cell — the size is chosen so the type fills its container: the rendered glyph bounding box must span at least 60% of the container's inner width where the layout fills horizontally, or at least 60% of its inner height where it fills vertically, measured in-page (glyph box vs container box) and recorded per surface in `docs/design-tokens.md`'s Type list as surface → face/size → container inner size → fill ratio. Bounded by no-overflow (no text clipped or spilling its container at 1440 or 390) and no-crowding (type never touches the container edge — the inset is at least the theme's own padding/rhythm token — and adjacent type runs never collide; the existing check.mjs collision/overflow findings govern). The step-6 screenshot check includes a dead-space item: a bubble, card, or tile whose largest empty band exceeds 25% of its minor dimension is a finding, fed to the fix batch like other checks. Flowing body copy is not a bubble — the C3 body-measure rule (65–75ch) governs there, not this rule. **Every font decision cites the type system (fix #17 F1 — extends fix #15 C1–C3, does not replace them).** Every face, weight, size, and tracking value on the page is an explicit value from the skills' type system — the dealt pairing (Taste step 4a / ui-ux-pro-max type scale), the theme file's type voice, or the `system.md` Type scale step. No face, weight, size, or tracking on the page is a browser or framework default, and no value is chosen flat (unstated, inherited, 'whatever it came with'). The Type list in `docs/design-tokens.md` gains two columns — weight and tracking — and every row gains a Source cell naming the exact skill text the choice comes from (pairing line, theme-file line, or scale step). A row with an empty Source cell, a Source of 'default', or a value that traces to no recorded pairing/scale/theme line is an incomplete build — a static check item in step 6, never a screen verdict. The polish-pass harmony check verifies the Type list including the Source cells (consistency only — a surface whose type does not trace to the recorded pairing/scale is a harmony divergence, recorded as candidate evidence, never a verdict). App surfaces record theirs the same way in `design-system/<project-slug>/MASTER.md`, conforming to the tokens file."),

The full F5 rule lives in `design-taste-frontend` step 4a."),

--- pattern: \bitalic\b
hackathon-ecc/skills/angular-developer/references/angular-aria.md:296:    <button ngToolbarWidget value="italic" class="tool-btn">I</button>
hackathon-ecc/skills/design-taste-frontend/SKILL.md:257:**Fonts render identically on iPhone and web — static bundled files only (fix #19 F2 — extends fix #15 C2).** The shipped font files are STATIC instances — one file per weight (e.g. 400/500/600/700/800), generated from a variable font with fontTools instancer/subsetter where the source is variable, with the optical size baked in (opsz) and no fvar table. A variable font file is NEVER the shipped delivery format: its interpolated weights render differently under iOS vs Windows, which is exactly the bug this rule fixes — a variable font may be the generation source, never a shipped file. NO system-ui / system fallback anywhere as a live path: no `system-ui` in any font stack, no bare generic `sans-serif`/`serif` as a live path — the stack names bundled faces only, and a bundled face that fails to load is a visible failure (a finding), never silently papered over by a system font. `font-synthesis: none` (no browser-synthesised bold/italic — the synthesised weights are the ones that diverge) and `text-size-adjust: 100%` (no platform text scaling). Preload the first-paint faces. The D1 screenshot QA (desktop + mobile) confirms the SAME faces render on both widths — compare the rendered faces, not just layout; a rendered face that is not a declared bundled face (a system font, a synthesised weight, a fallback sans) is a finding, the way the v1 audit caught Bowlby falling back to a plain sans. Where the stack's default font loading serves a variable font (e.g. next/font), the build instances it to static files before shipping. Recorded in `docs/design-tokens.md`'s Type list: each face → its static files (weight → file), source, and preloads. A Type-list row naming a variable file, a `system-ui` fallback, a bare generic fallback, or no preload is an incomplete build — a static check item in step 6, never a screen verdict.

SCOPE: the design-taste-frontend page system keeps its standing rule (`references/motion.md`: 'Scrolling moves nothing but the dealt moment') — scroll entrances do NOT apply to Taste landing pages under this rule. The step-6 check scrolls to each section, waits 1500ms, and confirms in the screenshot that no content is still hidden (no element left at opacity 0 or off-screen). A section still hidden after the wait is a finding, fed to the fix batch. build.sh:23249:   "\n\n**Default motion set (fix #17, v4-level).** Every page carries the default motion set — scroll entrances with a 1500ms fail-safe (M1/M2, on motion-governed surfaces; landing pages keep the Taste scroll ban except where a theme file records a scoped exception), hover micro-interactions on every interactive element (M3), life in existing pieces (M4), and at least one small interactive bit per page where the page has one (M5) — decided here for feel/timing, implemented with the motion-* skills. Reduced motion is respected in CSS and JS (M6). Each item is recorded in `docs/design-tokens.md`'s Motion list (surface → item → trigger/tokens/fail-safe).\n\n**M3 — Hover micro-interactions on every interactive element (fix #17).** Every link, button, linked card, and linked photograph has a visible hover state per the hover layer (`references/motion.md`, hover-layer table) and the motion defaults (enter: opacity + small translateY; press: scale 0.97; transitions scoped to the changed properties, never `transition: all`). hackathon-ecc/skills/design-taste-frontend/SKILL.md:265:**Every UI decision carries a thought-out reason across the FULL experience (fix #19 D19).** No silent defaults, no arbitrary picks. Each UI decision run through the skills — font, color, layout, element, treatment, motion, component, spacing — carries an explicit recorded reason in the decision record (`docs/design-tokens.md` / the decision record / `design-system/<project-slug>/MASTER.md` on app surfaces; the run ledger for the smallest flows): (1) what job it serves, (2) what was considered and rejected, (3) how it matches the theme. The reasoning must consider the FULL experience, not just the UI: every decision's recorded reason addresses SIX dimensions, one line each — **Motion** (what moves, what entrance/hover/loop it carries, or `none — <reason>`); **Theme** (how it fits the dealt theme — material, tokens, palette balance, do-nots); **Texture** (the not-flat-UI texture layer — what less-flat-ui treatment it carries, or `none — <reason>`); **Visuals** (imagery, iconography, visual treatment, what it looks like); **Techniques** (the other design techniques brought to bear — element kinds and skill techniques beyond the decision's own kind, e.g. a dealt moment, a diagram, an effect, a type moment, a craft-floor rule); **Colors** (palette tokens used, balance, contrast). A dimension that genuinely does not apply records `none — <reason>` — an explicit none, never a missing line. A decision whose recorded reasoning covers only layout/structure — the UI alone — does NOT satisfy this rule. A UI decision with no recorded reason, a missing dimension, or a layout-only reason is an incomplete build — a static check item in step 6, never a screen verdict. The audit/dry-run loop CHECKS the reasoning is present: the static checks verify every decision-record entry carries all six dimension lines, and the blind reviewer on UI diffs flags a missing dimension as a finding (minor at least). The check verifies the reasoning is PRESENT and addresses the dimensions — the quality of the reasoning itself is browser-qa's and the reviewer's judgment, not a static check.

hackathon-ecc/skills/polish-pass/reference/craft-floor.md:74:- [ ] Touch targets ≥ 44×44px (Fitts' law). When the visible mark is smaller, keep its size and enlarge only the hit area with an overlay: `position: relative` on the control and `::after { content: ""; position: absolute; inset: -8px; }` (pick the inset that reaches 44px). Don't use `min-height`/padding that bloats a visible chip or icon button. In wrapped rows and stacked lists, hit areas must not overlap: each side's overlay extends at most half the gap to the next target; if that can't reach 44px, widen the gap, not the mark.

=== D15 contrast 4.5 ===

hackathon-ecc/rules/react-native-accessibility.mdc:43:- Meet WCAG AA contrast: 4.5:1 for body text, 3:1 for large text and meaningful UI/graphical elements.
hackathon-ecc/rules/hackathon-mode.mdc:18:When building UI that is NOT a full landing or company page, use `ui-ux-pro-max` and `less-flat-ui` TOGETHER. `ui-ux-pro-max` sets the design direction; `less-flat-ui` adds the texture layer on any surface (backgrounds, cards, buttons, dividers, headings). Apply every `less-flat-ui` treatment that fits the surface — a treatment does not fit a dense dashboard, form, or data table, which stay flat. Contrast stays at 4.5:1 or better. Respect `prefers-reduced-motion`.

hackathon-ecc/skills/design-taste-frontend/SKILL.md:247:4a. **Define the design system before building any UI.** Before step 5, freeze the system on the plan (one line each) — this is a pre-UI step, not a gate: **visual identity** (the one idea the page looks like, in words); **typography + type scale** (the dealt pairing and tokens, `system.md` Type); The dealt pairing is the ONLY permitted faces on the build — no other face is introduced — and the pairing + its source are recorded in `docs/design-tokens.md` (fix #15 C1). Display faces are self-hosted as STATIC instances (one bundled woff2 file per weight + `@font-face` in the built page) whenever the face's character matters — a variable font file is never the shipped format, and the closest installed font is a failure, not a fallback; body faces are likewise static instances, and name their source (self-hosted static files + preloads) in `docs/design-tokens.md` (fix #19 F2, extending fix #15 C2). No `system-ui` or bare generic fallback anywhere as a live path; `font-synthesis: none` and `text-size-adjust: 100%`. This is a required build step, not a recommendation; checkable: the built page contains `@font-face` for the display face, and the tokens file names the source. **colour palette + semantic tokens** (ink, `--action`, `--g-main`/`--g-alt`/`--g-dark`, `--muted` — what each token MEANS, e.g. action, success, error, disabled — `system.md` Colour; no raw hex in components — gates.md 48/24a governs on landing); **spacing/layout system** (rhythm tokens + the 12-column grid, `system.md` Rhythm tokens); **component hierarchy** (which components exist, which are primary vs. secondary, one button pair / one card style / one radius scale — `system.md` Radius and buttons, Cards; gates.md F4/F15 governs on landing); **navigation / IA** (nav structure and section order — `system.md` Nav, `sections.md`); **responsive behaviour** (how each section stacks at 1024 / 768 / 390 / 320); **motion / micro-interactions** (hero entrance, hover/press, the dealt moments — house rule 5, `motion.md`; for motion beyond the page system's own entrance and moments — for example interactive app-frame widgets in React/Next.js — decide feel and timing with `make-interfaces-feel-better` first, then implement with the motion-* skills (`motion-foundations`, `motion-patterns`, `motion-advanced`)); **accessibility** (contrast 4.5:1, focus rings, keyboard order, reduced motion — `system.md` Access, checklist); **loading / empty / success / error states** for every interactive surface (what each state shows, in the page's own voice — a loading state never an empty ground, an empty state that says what to do next, an error under its own field, a success confirmed only after a real send). Then write the frozen system to `docs/design-tokens.md` in the project root (one line each, same items) — first two lines are the dealt Hallmark theme: its slug, its Tokens block values, and its palette balance, so a later slice briefing from this file alone can recover which theme (and its Range/material) the landing used — and this file is the durable single source of truth: later UI slices' briefs restate tokens from it, and the dashboard MASTER.md (`ui-ux-pro-max`) must conform to it; on divergence this file wins. If the project has no landing page, this step does not run and the first UI slice (`ui-ux-pro-max`) writes the file instead — see orchestrate's no-landing fallback. **Anti-generic principles:** never a generic AI-dashboard look — no excessive gradients, no grid of repetitive look-alike cards, no decorative clutter that carries no information; every choice is deliberate typography, spacing, alignment, and contrast, and a surface that adds nothing is cut. Anything already fixed by the deal above is named, not re-decided; only the missing pieces (notably the states and the semantic token meanings) are defined here. Also read the Impeccable craft floor (`skills/polish-pass/reference/impeccable-craft-floor.md` in the plugin) before building any UI: its bans are absolute as static/code-level checks — a pinned brief never overrides a ban — a ban is design guidance / static-check evidence, never a pass/fail verdict on a rendered screen (that verdict is `browser-qa`'s alone) — and where it and this skill disagree, the stricter ban wins — except that on a landing page Hallmark gate 2b's guarded single-hue gradient-text ramp is permitted (gates.md §4 governs there; the polish detector's gradient-text tell does not apply to it), and except page-level template patterns, which are candidate evidence for `browser-qa`, never a rewrite on this check alone. **Single source of truth on divergence:** this frozen landing system (recorded in `docs/design-tokens.md`) is the single source of truth for the whole product's design tokens. When the dashboard/app slice later records its system in `design-system/<project-slug>/MASTER.md` (ui-ux-pro-max), that MASTER.md must conform to this frozen system — same tokens, same type scale, same component decisions. If the two diverge, this frozen landing system wins; do not re-derive a competing system. **Each Range pattern's named lead element is its own element (fix #15 R5 — baseline: the Bento lead tile's lone figure was folded into the tile, not counted).** When a theme Range pattern names a lead/hero element (e.g. Sticker's Bento "the lead tile carries the lone figure"), that element is recorded as its own type moment in `docs/design-tokens.md`, distinct from the surface carrying it.

hackathon-ecc/skills/design-taste-frontend/SKILL.md:247:4a. **Define the design system before building any UI.** Before step 5, freeze the system on the plan (one line each) — this is a pre-UI step, not a gate: **visual identity** (the one idea the page looks like, in words); **typography + type scale** (the dealt pairing and tokens, `system.md` Type); The dealt pairing is the ONLY permitted faces on the build — no other face is introduced — and the pairing + its source are recorded in `docs/design-tokens.md` (fix #15 C1). Display faces are self-hosted as STATIC instances (one bundled woff2 file per weight + `@font-face` in the built page) whenever the face's character matters — a variable font file is never the shipped format, and the closest installed font is a failure, not a fallback; body faces are likewise static instances, and name their source (self-hosted static files + preloads) in `docs/design-tokens.md` (fix #19 F2, extending fix #15 C2). No `system-ui` or bare generic fallback anywhere as a live path; `font-synthesis: none` and `text-size-adjust: 100%`. This is a required build step, not a recommendation; checkable: the built page contains `@font-face` for the display face, and the tokens file names the source. **colour palette + semantic tokens** (ink, `--action`, `--g-main`/`--g-alt`/`--g-dark`, `--muted` — what each token MEANS, e.g. action, success, error, disabled — `system.md` Colour; no raw hex in components — gates.md 48/24a governs on landing); **spacing/layout system** (rhythm tokens + the 12-column grid, `system.md` Rhythm tokens); **component hierarchy** (which components exist, which are primary vs. secondary, one button pair / one card style / one radius scale — `system.md` Radius and buttons, Cards; gates.md F4/F15 governs on landing); **navigation / IA** (nav structure and section order — `system.md` Nav, `sections.md`); **responsive behaviour** (how each section stacks at 1024 / 768 / 390 / 320); **motion / micro-interactions** (hero entrance, hover/press, the dealt moments — house rule 5, `motion.md`; for motion beyond the page system's own entrance and moments — for example interactive app-frame widgets in React/Next.js — decide feel and timing with `make-interfaces-feel-better` first, then implement with the motion-* skills (`motion-foundations`, `motion-patterns`, `motion-advanced`)); **accessibility** (contrast 4.5:1, focus rings, keyboard order, reduced motion — `system.md` Access, checklist); **loading / empty / success / error states** for every interactive surface (what each state shows, in the page's own voice — a loading state never an empty ground, an empty state that says what to do next, an error under its own field, a success confirmed only after a real send). Then write the frozen system to `docs/design-tokens.md` in the project root (one line each, same items) — first two lines are the dealt Hallmark theme: its slug, its Tokens block values, and its palette balance, so a later slice briefing from this file alone can recover which theme (and its Range/material) the landing used — and this file is the durable single source of truth: later UI slices' briefs restate tokens from it, and the dashboard MASTER.md (`ui-ux-pro-max`) must conform to it; on divergence this file wins. If the project has no landing page, this step does not run and the first UI slice (`ui-ux-pro-max`) writes the file instead — see orchestrate's no-landing fallback. **Anti-generic principles:** never a generic AI-dashboard look — no excessive gradients, no grid of repetitive look-alike cards, no decorative clutter that carries no information; every choice is deliberate typography, spacing, alignment, and contrast, and a surface that adds nothing is cut. Anything already fixed by the deal above is named, not re-decided; only the missing pieces (notably the states and the semantic token meanings) are defined here. Also read the Impeccable craft floor (`skills/polish-pass/reference/impeccable-craft-floor.md` in the plugin) before building any UI: its bans are absolute as static/code-level checks — a pinned brief never overrides a ban — a ban is design guidance / static-check evidence, never a pass/fail verdict on a rendered screen (that verdict is `browser-qa`'s alone) — and where it and this skill disagree, the stricter ban wins — except that on a landing page Hallmark gate 2b's guarded single-hue gradient-text ramp is permitted (gates.md §4 governs there; the polish detector's gradient-text tell does not apply to it), and except page-level template patterns, which are candidate evidence for `browser-qa`, never a rewrite on this check alone. **Single source of truth on divergence:** this frozen landing system (recorded in `docs/design-tokens.md`) is the single source of truth for the whole product's design tokens. When the dashboard/app slice later records its system in `design-system/<project-slug>/MASTER.md` (ui-ux-pro-max), that MASTER.md must conform to this frozen system — same tokens, same type scale, same component decisions. If the two diverge, this frozen landing system wins; do not re-derive a competing system. **Each Range pattern's named lead element is its own element (fix #15 R5 — baseline: the Bento lead tile's lone figure was folded into the tile, not counted).** When a theme Range pattern names a lead/hero element (e.g. Sticker's Bento "the lead tile carries the lone figure"), that element is recorded as its own type moment in `docs/design-tokens.md`, distinct from the surface carrying it.

hackathon-ecc/skills/polish-pass/reference/lenses/animate.md:3:Motion explains state, relationship, and feedback: it tells a Persona "I heard you", "this came from there", and "this changed", closing the feedback loop of every action. Decoration without a job is animation debt, and motion that stutters makes the whole product feel cheap. The more often someone sees a motion, the less of it there should be.
